14.1   The next discriminating measurement
The proposed question is whether independently measured demand and route scaling predict throughput
against resource inventory on held-out networks. It is an unrun design proposal. No apparatus, supplier
quote or adequately powered sample has been established here, and it adds no commitment to an existing
funded project.
   1. Define an eligible transport apparatus or dataset with independent measurements of throughput,
      inventory, size, route lengths and speed. State the observational unit and exclusions. Do not
      manufacture an inventory observation from the accounting equation being evaluated.
   2. Fix the size range and one routing intervention. For a declared increase of every served route length
      by the same factor 𝑐 > 1, with demands and speeds unchanged and no extra waiting, inventory
      should increase by the factor 𝑐 at each size; the exponent need not change. A claim about a changed
      exponent instead needs an intervention that changes the size dependence of routes or another
      specified scaling premise. State which comparison is being tested, and establish geometry before
      fitting the response.
   3. Split complete independent networks into calibration and held-out sets. Use calibration records
      to specify the finite-range functions for demand, route length and speed, including any offset,
      curvature or finite-size correction. Lock their joint uncertainty and the predicted held-out trajectory.
      An exponent-only prediction is admissible only if the protocol justifies a power approximation and
      a bound on its error over the actual size range (Equation (18)).
   4. Measure held-out throughput and inventory independently of their predicted values. With the
      declared steady-flow premises, the finite-range prediction is 𝑁 (𝐿) = 𝐵(𝐿) ℓ¯𝑞 (𝐿)/𝑣(𝐿), with all
      three right-hand functions fixed from calibration. Model the measurement errors and any shared
      calibration covariance. Under justified power behaviour and uniform fixed speed, the limiting
      target exponent is 𝜇/(𝜇 + 𝜁). For a separately specified uniform-speed scaling 𝑣 ∝ 𝐿 𝜐 , it becomes
      𝜇/(𝜇 + 𝜁 − 𝜐) when the denominator is positive. Do not fit a missing mechanism variable after
      seeing a discrepancy.
   5. Compare the predicted model with a free-exponent power law and a justified curved alternative on
      the same response and likelihood. Prespecify measurement-error treatment, uncertainty, exclusions
      and multiplicity. The choice between a log-transformed fit and nonlinear fitting must follow the
      assumed error distribution, rather than a universal preference for one method (Xiao et al. 2011). Use
      design analysis to establish sample size and a meaningful discrepancy tolerance before validation;
      inadequate precision returns the declared inconclusive outcome, not a post-hoc relabelling of the
      study.
   6. Publish the prediction, observations, deviations and all planned comparisons. A premise that fails is
      itself a limitation to report. A precise predictive failure cannot be repaired by changing dimension,
      the outcome or the model after inspection.
    The accounting identity is mathematical. The empirical questions are whether its non-definitional
premises and measurement bridges apply, whether independently estimated parameters transfer, and
whether the prediction beats serious alternatives. Agreement would support that specified mechanism
over the tested range. It would establish no universal metabolic law or AI alignment guarantee.

Standing covenant. Prove this paper wrong, and I will publish the refutation myself. Falsification
conditions are stated in this paper; the standing challenge: github.com/MichaelDariusEastwood/arc-scali
ng-challenge. The earlier dated predictions remain available beside the corrected argument.

15         Making the composition claim measurable
The useful chain is from a specified observable and mechanism to a locked prediction. A family label
alone does not supply an exponent; a repeated numerical fraction does not establish a common mechanism.
The homomorphism result, the transport accounting and the capability ODE answer separate conditional
questions. This section sets out what a test must supply to connect them to observations.

       1. Declare what is measured. Specify the response, input, units and both composition operations.
       ⇓    Classical mathematics constrains a family, given its premises.
       2. Specify the physical model. Measure demand, routes and speed; justify the inventory or mass bridge.
       ⇓    The transport assumptions select an exponent for a particular variable pair.
       3. Test a locked prediction. Use held-out systems and independently measured outcomes, against
       prespecified alternatives.

Figure 1. From a conditional mathematical statement to an empirical claim. A theorem does not supply the physical
premises; calibration and validation must not recover the same constructed identity. No observed data are plotted.


Definition 4 (A measured baseline). For a positive response 𝑈 and a declared reference input 𝑅0 , define
the baseline and normalised response by
                                                               𝑈 (𝑅)
                              𝐼 = 𝑈 (𝑅0 ) > 0,      𝐺 (𝑅) =          ,       𝐺 (𝑅0 ) = 1.                       (47)
                                                                 𝐼
The response, its units, the reference condition and the population to which it applies are part of the
definition. 𝑅0 = 1 denotes a first-pass baseline when that convention is appropriate. If the baseline is
zero, this ratio is undefined and a different, prospectively specified measurement is needed.
    This baseline is not automatically entropy reduction in bits, intelligence or self-correction capacity.
Accuracy, logical error, metabolic rate and phase coherence are different observables. A common symbol
cannot supply a conversion between them. Information-theoretic quantities require a specified random
variable and probability distribution; energy converted into useful performance requires a physical and
measurement model. Landauer’s lower bound on irreversible erasure does not make every energy flow a
measure of information gained (Landauer 1961).

15.1       The assumptions to fix before validation
   1. Observable and axis. Name the response, units, baseline, admissible values and input. Distinguish
      additive passes, multiplicative scale ratios, code distance, qubit count, organism mass and elapsed
      time. State the transformation used before interpreting a slope.
   2. Both operations. State the input operation and output operation and why the observable is expected
      to preserve them. Separate that equation from the state-update rule. If the operation is Equation (45),
      test that operation rather than silently replacing it by ordinary multiplication of depth.
   3. Exact, approximate or asymptotic claim. Name which is proposed. For an approximate identity
      give the residual norm, domain and error bound. For a scaling limit state the ratio, limiting variable
      and possible slowly varying factor. Finite observations test a specified finite-range consequence;
      they do not verify every point of a global hypothesis.
   4. Exponent-selecting assumptions. In the transport model, measure demand scaling 𝜇 and flow-
      weighted path scaling 𝜁; justify a demand-based assignment 𝜇 = 𝑑 and any bridge from transport to
      mass. In the gain ODE, identify 𝑎 and 𝛽 𝐿 independently and predict the complete shifted trajectory.

                                                          30
                             The origin of scaling laws in recursive composition


      Neither dimension nor coupling may be inferred from the validation exponent and then presented
      as its prediction.
   5. Resource and clock controls. Identify which weights, code, memory, prompts, tools and evaluation
      procedures change. Record hardware, total computation, latency and external help. A change in
      resources or cycle duration must not be mistaken for the claimed improvement mechanism.
   6. A discriminating outcome. State which observation would favour or reject this conditional
      explanation against realistic alternatives. Specify the uncertainty, the error model, dependence
      between observations, saturation and the precision needed for an inconclusive result. An intervention
      on routing, information access or the improvement rule is stronger evidence of mechanism than
      another fit to the same curve.

15.2   An independent prediction needs separate information
Assign complete systems or runs to calibration and validation before inspecting outcomes. Use calibration
to justify the mechanism and estimate its parameters. Lock the predicted trajectory, uncertainty, variable
definitions, exclusions, stopping rule and decision margins before validation. Compare exact and shifted
powers, exponential, logarithmic and appropriate bounded or broken-form rivals on the same response
with compatible likelihoods. A repeated observation from one system is not an independent system, and a
parameter obtained by transforming the fitted validation slope is not an independent prediction.
     For the network question, independently measured demand and route scaling should predict later
throughput against transport quantity; a routing intervention has a specified effect on the whole predicted
curve. Multiplying route lengths by a size-independent factor while holding demand and speed fixed
changes inventory by that factor, but need not change an exponent. An exponent-changing intervention
must instead alter a scaling premise. Other declared controls remain fixed. For a capability question,
the validation task must distinguish improved task performance from an improved ability to produce
subsequent improvements. These are design requirements, not new results or a registration made by this
paper.
     Report departures from the premises and failed predictions rather than moving a system to another
class after seeing the answer. A useful diagnostic can motivate a new prospective test; it cannot turn
the original failure into success. Analytic identities, retrospective fits and prospective results retain
their different evidential roles. The existing withdrawal of Prediction 2 and every frozen registration
remain unchanged. The programme’s laws remain laws under test, with verdicts taken only through their
registered rules. No phase diagram of all complexity, universal physical exponent limit or cosmological
ladder is needed to carry out this measurement programme.


16     What this paper does and does not show
The paper establishes conditional mathematical statements and a prospective way to test their applicability.
The four chosen homomorphism problems yield classical function families. The transport calculation
yields an exponent only under explicit demand and path scalings; mass and inventory require further
bridges. The numerical resemblance of exponents across fields supplies no additional proof.
    Its biological comparisons are source-specific, its cosmological map is analytic, and its AI summaries
retain the limitations of their own reports. The former pooled biological inferences, universal dimension-
only bound and physical complexity ladder are superseded. Prediction 2 remains withdrawn. No new
observation or registered verdict is reported in this revision.

17     Limitations
     • The exact composition identities and global stability assumptions require independent justification.
       A finite noisy fit cannot establish them across an unobserved domain.
     • The transport exponent is asymptotic; finite-range prediction requires a full curve or a justified
       bound on finite-size corrections. It depends on demand, paths, speed and the chosen response. A
       measured spatial dimension alone supplies none of those extra assumptions. Body mass needs a
       physiological bridge.
     • The biological estimates concern different taxa, conditions and variables. The fungal factors cannot
       be pooled into one metabolic relation. Selected published exponents do not establish a universal
       law.
     • The cosmological reparametrisation has no independent mechanistic content. Photon trajectories
       and a cosmic web are not assumptions of the homogeneous background solution.
     • Earlier computational and behavioural results retain their design, selection, scoring and amendment
       limitations. This revision reruns none of them and claims no new confirmation.
     • The prospective transport test still needs an eligible apparatus or dataset, practical resource estimates,
       independent replication units and a design-sensitivity analysis before registration.
     • The synthesis relies on established mathematical and transport literature. Its value must be
       demonstrated through a useful distinction or successful prospective prediction; a larger catalogue
       of matching fractions would not establish that contribution.


18     Conclusion
A useful scaling prediction needs more than a familiar curve. It needs declared variables, a justified
composition or transport model, and a test on observations that did not select the assumptions. The



                                                       32
                              The origin of scaling laws in recursive composition


classical equations constrain families; the conditional transport model shows where an exponent enters.
Neither result classifies all of nature.
    The corrected biological and cosmological examples show why these distinctions matter. Heterogen-
eous measured slopes cannot supply one pooled validation, and an algebraic change of variables cannot
supply a physical mechanism. Fixed computational rules can generate superlinear trajectories, while
correction and safety require additional quantities and models.
    The next empirical contribution is the prospective transport comparison: estimate the mechanism
separately, seal the prediction and allow the held-out observations to support it, contradict it or remain
inconclusive.

      “One may say ‘the eternal mystery of the world is its comprehensibility.’ ”
      Albert Einstein (1936)

    Whether much of what the universe does can be described with a few composition operators, which
would be part of why it is comprehensible, is not shown here. The mathematics above does not deny it
and is offered as evidence for nothing else; only declared operators that predict held-out observations, as
the test of Section 14.1 would, could begin to show it.

                                             Raise AI with care.

Code and data. No study or data analysis was run for this revision. The historical suite arc_comp
lete_test_suite.py and its original results remain byte for byte in the dated public repository. It
implements earlier computations, including the pooled and three-group analyses withdrawn here; it is not
an implementation or validation of the new prospective protocol. The paper’s existing deposit identity
and earlier editions are retained.

A     Version history and corrections
This appendix separates the present revision from the earlier dated record. A correction is dated when it
is made; retaining an earlier passage does not make its present correction part of the earlier claim. Table 8
records the changes in version 3.14. The subsequent history preserves what the earlier editions said,
including their claims and the limits of their corrections.

A.1    Version 3.14: revised 1 November 2026
Version 3.14 is dated 1 November 2026. It reports no new data, registration, study run or empirical
result. The earlier version remains intact. Its content is retained through the current argument, the
source-measurement and companion records, the explicitly historical material and Appendix I, except
three cosmological passages kept out for the reason Table 8 gives; superseded interpretations are not
silently recast as earlier statements of the new conditional argument.
    Several corrections below were already made in version 3.13: the restricted abstract, the distinction
between metabolic scaling and capability against depth, the Friedmann boundary calculation, the
withdrawal of mixed error aggregates, and the withdrawal of Prediction 2. The table below distinguishes
those retained corrections from the further changes made in this revision.

reported estimate and uncertainty is retained. It supplies no numerical estimate of the capability-gain,
correction-capacity or cycle-time parameters. Appendix H restores the detailed alignment and pilot
reports, the failed run, the earlier heartbeat illustration, the superseded pooled and phase interpretations,
the code provenance and all three predecessor figures that were preserved in source records. Each is
labelled with its date and scope; no withdrawn inference is restored as a current finding.

How to read the retained history below. The following record is carried forward from the version 3.13
manuscript of 30 October 2026; version 3.13 was released with the edition date 31 October 2026 and
the further changes listed above. Its wording is historical: “this version”, “now”, result grades, novelty
statements and registration status refer to the edition or date named there, not to the present version.
References inside that retained record use the section, equation, table, figure and appendix numbers
printed in version 3.13, unless a passage itself names an earlier edition. The original reference numbers
are fixed here so that a historical pointer cannot silently lead to a changed current equation or diagram. In
particular, Figures 1, 2 and 3 below are figures of version 3.13, not current figures. Table 9 retains the
mapping from version 3.12 to version 3.13; it is not a map into version 3.14. Current claims and status
are given in the main text and Section 16.

A.2    Version 3.13
v3.13, 30 October 2026: Substantive. Prediction 2 is withdrawn: the published exponents of loss
against parameters for language models, and the measured fall of the exponent as the data’s dimension
rises, refute it. Section 14 now says so, gives the measurements and credits the earlier work on the
exponent and the dimension of the data manifold (Sharma and Kaplan 2020; Bahri et al. 2021). Level 6
of Section 12 no longer calls the prediction untested, and a dated correction note records what both
passages printed before. The works of Sharma and Kaplan (2020), Bahri et al. (2021), Hoffmann et al.
(2022), Besiroglu et al. (2024) and Pope et al. (2021) are added and Kaplan et al. (2020) is now cited. No
registered law, registered prediction, kill condition or dependency changes; no other result is added or
changed by the withdrawal; no date of any claim moves.
    This version is also the paper’s edition on the programme’s presentation standard, typeset in LaTeX.
The sections of version 3.12 keep their order, except that Section 12, What Is New, becomes Section 2;
Table 16 maps every earlier section to its place here. The title now names the paper’s domain, recursive
composition, as the scope correction of 22 August 2026 requires; until this version it read “Framework

• The boundary 𝑤 = −1/3 is no longer called the boundary between power-law and exponential
  growth: under the change of variables it is where expansion turns from decelerating to accelerating
  and 𝛼 → 1; past it the expansion is an accelerating power law, exponential only at 𝑤 = −1; and in
  Cauchy’s classification 𝑑 → ∞ is the linear limit of the power law (Sections 2, 9, 12, 13 and 15).

A.3     Earlier corrections and notes
Correction note, 30 October 2026. Section 14 printed Prediction 2 as: “The neural scaling law
exponent (loss vs parameters in machine learning) should equal 𝑑/(𝑑 + 1) where 𝑑 is the intrinsic
dimension of the training data manifold.” Level 6 of Section 12 called it untested; the published record
had already tested it. The exponents of loss against parameters published for language models from 2020
lie below one half, the least value 𝑑/(𝑑 + 1) takes once 𝑑 reaches 1, and in controlled experiments the
measured exponent falls as the data’s dimension rises, where 𝑑/(𝑑 + 1) rises. The prediction is withdrawn.
The earlier work on this exponent and the dimension of the data manifold is credited where the prediction
stood, and Kaplan et al. (2020), listed until now without a citation, is cited there. No other result of this
paper changes with it, and nothing already withdrawn is restored.

The abstract of version 3.12, as printed. Scope correction, 22 August 2026. An earlier version of
this abstract opened by asserting that every scaling law in nature follows one of exactly three mathematical
forms, and that this paper proves the form is uniquely determined. Both statements were stronger
than this paper supports, and stronger than its own body claims. They are withdrawn here and the
corrected statement follows. Where a recursive amplification process has a well defined composition
operator on its input and output, and satisfies the regularity conditions Cauchy’s functional equations
require, the admissible functional forms are constrained to a small named family. That is a conditional
mathematical classification, not a survey of nature: it does not establish that every empirical scaling
process is such a homomorphism, and the bounded family’s exhaustiveness remains an open problem, as
Paper VII states. The grid is four-celled rather than three, the fourth cell being the logarithmic family.
This paper’s own scope condition, stated below and unchanged, is that the relation applies wherever a
𝑑-dimensional hierarchical network partitions space and fails where such networks are absent, which is by
itself incompatible with any claim about every scaling law in nature. A single formula, 𝛼 = 𝑑/(𝑑 + 1),
predicts scaling exponents across biology (9 species groups; species-group count pending recompute
per canonical facts register), physics (4 domains catalogued after retiering; the below-0.2% mean-error
aggregate is WITHDRAWN: a mean over analytic identities and unsourced reference values is small by
construction and is not evidence of agreement with nature; the earthquake row has been deleted as a
definitional identity per section 13), and cosmology (2 eras, exact), including the derivation of mammalian
heart rate scaling from first principles. The formula is identified as a network-partition identity: it
applies wherever a 𝑑-dimensional hierarchical network partitions space, and fails where such networks
are absent. A geometric proof shows that all physical scaling exponents are constrained below 1 by the
finite dimensionality of space - not by energy dissipation, but by geometry itself. Intelligence, operating
through recursive self-reference in abstract information space, is identified as the first natural phenomenon
to break this geometric scaling bound. The Friedmann equation for cosmic expansion is shown to be



                                                      49
                             The origin of scaling laws in recursive composition


a special case under the mapping 𝑑 = 2/(1 + 3𝑤), with the deceleration/acceleration boundary from
general relativity coinciding exactly with the power-law/exponential boundary from Cauchy’s theorem.

The reading note of version 3.12, as printed. How to read this paper. This paper traces the scaling
forms behind Law I to their mathematical origin. It identifies 𝛼 = 𝑑/(𝑑 + 1) as a network-partition
identity that applies wherever a 𝑑-dimensional hierarchical network partitions space, argues that geometry
constrains classical scaling exponents below 1, and marks intelligence operating through recursive
self-reference as the first natural phenomenon to break the geometric bound. Its place within the theory is
the origin story of scaling itself, upstream of the AI-side measurements. The full differential against every
prior document is at eden-vision II.A.8.

Scope note, 19 September 2026 (Section 2 of version 3.12). An owner review of this paragraph finds
that it restates in the body the universal claim this paper’s abstract withdrew on 22 August 2026, in the
words ‘everywhere’ and ‘every known scaling law is one of these three’, and that it counts saturation among
forms Section 3 places outside the classification. The corrected reading follows, and the classification
itself is untouched. What is established is conditional: where a recursive process has a well defined
composition operator carrying one of the two structures on the positive reals into one of the two structures,
and the regularity conditions Cauchy’s equations require are satisfied, the admissible functional forms are
constrained to a small named family. Section 3 states that family as four-celled, the fourth cell being the
logarithmic one, and says in terms that it does not exhaust scaling laws, because most systems are not
homomorphisms of either structure. Saturation is among the behaviours it does not cover: it arises from
bounded dynamics (finite carrying capacity, mass action, receptor occupancy, depletion), and Section 3
records that presenting it as a Cauchy family is a category error. Raising the count from three to four
would not repair this paragraph, because the defect lies in the range of its quantifiers, which run over
all scaling processes while the result runs over homomorphisms under regularity; the repair reaches the
quantifier and the domain, not the numeral. Unchanged: the four-cell classification, which is a genuine
completeness statement about homomorphisms; the abstract’s dated scope correction, which this note
follows rather than softens; the placement of saturation in bounded dynamics; this section’s own opening
paragraph, which already states the classification conditionally, as a classification of admissible functions
under those conditions and not a survey of nature, and which the anchored sentence contradicts; and
Section 15’s own later statement that bounded growth is a separate, capped case rather than a solution of
those equations, which is already right and is the form this note follows.

Inference note, 19 September 2026 (Section 3 of version 3.12). An owner review of this sentence finds
that its first explanation draws from stability a conclusion the box immediately above has just said stability
cannot supply, and this note states the boundary that box sets. Hyers-Ulam stability is per-equation: a
function approximately satisfying one of the functional equations lies near an exact solution of that same
equation, and a function satisfying none of them is not drawn toward any. Stability within an equation
therefore says nothing about how often systems in nature approximately satisfy that equation in the first
place, which is what dominance in nature would require, and which the box above names as a separate
empirical question this theorem cannot settle. What the loopholes do explain is preserved and is not
in question: why real data shows small deviations, the assumptions being only approximately satisfied,
and where to look for new physics, wherever an assumption is systematically violated. Unchanged: the
Hyers-Ulam statement itself and its per-equation scope; the withdrawal of the earlier attractor framing
recorded immediately above; and the other two explanations this sentence offers, which stand as printed.

Note on the box withdrawn on 11 August 2026 (Section 7 of version 3.12). This box previously
read “The Atomic Bomb = Einstein + ARC” and stated that the ARC Principle explains why the chain
reaction is exponential, concluding that neither Einstein’s equation nor this framework alone is a bomb
but together they are. That was withdrawn on 11 August 2026. It borrowed the authority of a result with
several independent derivations for a framework resting on one assumption, and it presented a coordinate
classification as a mechanism.

Note on the passage corrected on 11 August 2026 (Section 8 of version 3.12). This passage previously
read: “This is not an analogy. It is not a metaphor. It is an algebraic identity. . . Every cosmological era,
without exception, is a specific case of 𝛼 = 𝑑/(𝑑 + 1).” It was corrected on 11 August 2026 because it
presented identity as the strongest available evidence when identity is in fact the weakest evidential tier,
and because it contradicted the provenance-classified catalogue in this same paper, which already records
those cosmology rows as analytic identities rather than measurements.

Correspondence note, 19 September 2026 (Section 12 of version 3.12). An owner review of the fourth
entry above, on the expansion of the universe, finds that the agreement it reports follows from the change
of variables it states, and this note says what the correspondence establishes and what establishing more
would take. The mapping 𝑑 = 2/(1 + 3𝑤) is an invertible change of variables between an equation-of-state
parameter and a dimension parameter. Under such a map the image of a boundary is the boundary of the
image, so the coincidence of the cosmic boundary with the Cauchy boundary is a property of the map
rather than a second derivation arriving at the same place by its own route: two derivations agree when
each reaches its conclusion without recourse to the other, and here one quantity is rewritten as the other.
The correspondence may still carry content, and this note does not say it does not; what it says is that the
content has to come from something the map does not supply, either an argument that the two parameters
measure the same physical quantity, or a prediction that survives the change of variables and is then
checked against data not used to fix the map. The same review finds that the seventh entry above, on the
space of classical laws, carries in this list the cardinality and scope claim that the note at the consequence
for physics in Section 15 addresses, and that note governs this entry too: a family whose members carry
a continuously varying coefficient and a continuously varying exponent is not a finite set, so what is
defensible is that the admissible forms are finitely many and the family they generate is finite-dimensional;
and the step from the classification of homomorphisms under regularity to all possible classical physical
laws is the step this paper’s abstract withdrew on 22 August 2026, because it requires that every physical
law be such a homomorphism, which Section 3 says is not generally so. The count of forms is not what is
at issue in either place: the repair reaches the quantifier and the domain, not the numeral. Prior art is
not assessed here: this list also carries universal novelty statements, no search was made in the review
that produced this note, no novelty verdict is recorded either way, and the silence is evidence neither
of priority nor of its absence. Unchanged: the exponent relation itself; its derivation by the groups
Section 11 lists, each of which reached it without reference to this programme; the cosmological entry as
a worked correspondence; and Section 12’s own statement that a formal proof of the sufficiency of the
three conditions does not yet exist and is the most important open problem in this programme.

finds that it restates in the body the universal claim this paper’s abstract withdrew on 22 August 2026, in
the words ‘There are no other options’, and that it counts a saturation curve among the forms Cauchy’s
equations produce, which Section 3 names a category error. The corrected reading follows, and the
classification itself is untouched. What is established is conditional: where a recursive process has a well
defined composition operator carrying one of the two structures on the positive reals into one of the two

structures, and the regularity conditions Cauchy’s equations require are satisfied, the admissible functional
forms are constrained to a small named family. Section 3 states that family as four-celled, the fourth cell
being the logarithmic one, and says in terms that it does not exhaust scaling laws, because most systems
are not homomorphisms of either structure. Saturation is among the behaviours it does not cover: it arises
from bounded dynamics (finite carrying capacity, mass action, receptor occupancy, depletion), and lies

Cardinality and scope note, 19 September 2026 (Section 15 of version 3.12). An owner review of
the box above finds two distinct overstatements in it, and this note states what the result gives in place of
each. The first is cardinality. A family whose members carry a continuously varying coefficient and a
continuously varying exponent has uncountably many members, since each value of the exponent already
gives a distinct member and the exponent ranges over an interval of the reals; the box names that dial itself.
A finite-dimensional family is not a finite set, so the defensible statement is that the admissible forms are
finitely many and that the family they generate is finite-dimensional, carrying a discrete form label and
two continuous parameters, rather than that the space of laws is not infinite. On the count, a label together
with two real parameters is ordinarily described as a two-parameter family of each of finitely many forms;
‘three-parameter’ counts the discrete label alongside the two continuous parameters, which is a naming
choice rather than an error and is recorded only because it sits in the sentence under repair. The second
overstatement is scope, and it is the one the note in Section 2 records. What is provable, and provable
without data, is the classification of homomorphisms under regularity: where the composition operator
carries one of the two structures on the positive reals into one of the two structures and the regularity
conditions hold, the admissible functional forms are constrained to a small named family, four-celled as
Section 3 states, with saturation lying outside it as a bounded case. The step from that classification to all
possible classical physical laws is the step the abstract withdrew on 22 August 2026, because it requires
that every physical law be such a homomorphism, which this paper does not establish and which Section
3 says is not generally so. Unchanged: the genuine content of the box, that the admissible functional
forms are constrained to a small named family under the stated conditions, which is the abstract’s own
corrected statement and survives this note entire; and the observation that the constraint follows from
Cauchy’s classification rather than from measurement.

Status note, 19 September 2026 (Section 15 of version 3.12). An owner review of the paragraph
above finds that it repeats the categorical form of the preceding box without the status marker that box
carries, and this note carries the marker here. The relation is a proposed invariant relation under test,
conditional on the three sufficiency conditions Section 12 names (multiplicative composition, space-filling
and conservation), a formal proof of whose sufficiency does not yet exist and which Section 12 names as
the most important open problem in this programme. Read under that condition the paragraph stands
as printed: given the three conditions, the exponent is determined and no other value is available to the
mechanism. Unchanged: the distinction the paragraph draws between a mechanism explanation and a
form explanation, which is its point and which this note does not touch; and the status marker in the
preceding box, which this note extends rather than replaces.

What the dates establish. This record distinguishes dates written inside documents, dates recorded by
deposit servers and commit dates supplied by committers. Each entry keeps its stated evidence class. A
dated claim is evidence of what was recorded and when; its mathematical validity and any priority over
earlier work require the corresponding argument and source comparison. Version 3.14 changes none of
the dates below.

composition equation A relation preserved by a specified observable under declared input and output
      operations. A state-update rule is a separate object.
homomorphism A map preserving the operations of its source and target structures. The four continuous
      group cases here include both the additive reals and the multiplicative positive reals.
regular variation A scale-ratio limit that permits a slowly varying factor; it does not assert an exact
      finite-range power.
conditional transport exponent The exponent implied by the demand, route and measurement assump-
      tions in Section 6; dimension alone is insufficient.
independent measurement An observation not calculated from the identity it is offered to validate.
      Statistical dependence between measurements must still be modelled.
analytic identity A consequence of definitions or assumptions. It is not an empirical success, even when
      its numerical value matches another identity.
capability baseline The positive measured response at a declared reference condition, not a conversion
      from energy or entropy into intelligence.
ARC Bound The conjectured value two associated with Law III under its additional correction-scaling
      premises, not a universal physical or safety bound.
laws under test The programme’s named claims, whose verdicts are governed by their registered
      procedures. Conditional mathematics alone supplies no empirical verdict.
preregistered by timestamp; registered A design fixed before data with its timestamp and receipt; an
      OSF registration submitted by the author and accepted by the registry. These records retain their
      respective evidential strengths.


D       The paper’s place in the programme
D.1      Which questions this paper informs
Within Recursive Dynamics, the field the author founded, this framework paper informs Law I, the ARC
Principle, a law under test. Its subject is whether a composition description and independently justified
mechanism can predict scaling. It develops the mathematical and measurement questions relevant to P9
and P18 of the theory registration; it is not a registered deciding test of either. The registered statements
remain unchanged. The Cauchy mathematics and earlier transport results retain their attribution; the
synthesis and prospective protocol are the contribution developed here. In the theory’s domain statement
this paper works the composed tier: where the composition of a system’s steps can be declared, the
mathematics of form reaches everything that composes, stars included, and it constrains the shape of their
scaling laws, never their raising; Laws I to III themselves govern only what runs under a corrector.
    Section 11 distinguishes the capability ODE from the correction model and the conditional pacing
relation of Law III. The physical examples derive none of those AI models. Prediction 5 retains a separate


                                                        61
                              The origin of scaling laws in recursive composition


behavioural question concerning the Eden Protocol. The paper makes no claim about the legal validity of
the HARI Treaty, the Draft Treaty on Hardware-Aligned Recursive Intelligence.

D.2    Persistence and placement
Laws IV and V are stated with their current qualifications in Section 16. Under version 1.104 of the theory
registration, Law IV has two limbs: its retention limb is the registered claim of proposition P15, word for
word, and its comparison limb has no registered proposition yet; Law V carries propositions P12 and P21.
The retention question under P15, the build-order question under P12 and the placement question under
P21 are different measurements. The programme’s Laws IV and V: the mathematics, version 1.2, revised
29 October 2026, supplies their mathematical account. The earlier note of 3 October 2026 is retained as
dated history in Appendix G; its then-current status words are not present-day assertions.

D.3    Companions and their distinct jobs
Paper I states Law I. The Foundational Paper gives the cross-domain framework. Paper VII treats
composition classification and its empirical comparison; its result depends on its candidate set, scoring
rules and historical protocol. It cannot establish a universal classification of nature. Paper III develops the
AI correction question. Paper X supplies the conditional drift/correction model, and Paper XIII supplies
the separate depth-to-time model. Each must be read with its own assumptions.
    The reported experiments and evaluation limitations are in Paper II, Paper IV.a, Paper IV.b, Paper IV.c
and Paper IV.d. The engineering and behavioural strands are developed in Paper V, Paper VI, Paper VIII,
Eden Engineering and Eden Vision. Paper IX, the Executive Summary and the Master Table of Contents
describe the broader programme. These links provide context, not cumulative evidence for this paper’s
transport model.

D.4    Reading the dated records
The reference list distinguishes each cited edition’s revision date from its first-publication record.
Appendix B records the timestamp strength of the author’s earlier claims. Historical priority establishes
what was recorded and when, not correctness or priority over a classical result. The fuller development
record and superseded formulations are preserved with the previous editions and the explicit correction
record; none is silently upgraded to a discovery of the narrower statements first made in this revision. The
full differential of this paper against every prior document of the programme is at Eden Vision, section
II.A.8; the reading note of version 3.12 is kept in Appendix A.3.


E     The contribution beside the closest earlier work
This appendix states the contribution at the scope supported by the comparisons in Section 2. The
mathematical ingredients have prior sources. What this paper develops within Recursive Dynamics, for
Law I, the ARC Principle, a law under test, is a route from a declared composition equation through
a specified transport model to a prospective empirical prediction. It separates those stages so that an
identity, an assumed mechanism and an observation cannot count as one another.
    The targeted source comparison includes established functional-equation and regular-variation work,
earlier transport derivations and their limitations. It is not an exhaustive search of every scaling literature.
In particular, Ostaszewski (2016) already connects homomorphisms, generalised operations and regular
variation; the author preprint precedes this programme’s paper, and the article was first published online
in 2015. Bingham et al. (1987) supplies the established regular-variation framework. The connection

between homomorphisms and scaling is therefore credited as prior work, rather than presented as a first

F       Source-specific biological and physical comparisons
The observations below retain their source, measured variables and method. They are published estimates
from different investigations, not one dataset collected to test the transport model. Source-supported
observations, analytic relations and historical claims awaiting verification are distinguished. No pooled
exponent, accuracy score or new statistical test is reported.

F.1     Metabolic estimates retain their measurement conditions
Table 13 preserves four source-specific comparisons from version 3.13. Their source readings are
documented in that edition’s reference and change records. These point estimates do not come with a
common error model or sampling frame; their proximity to a selected fraction is not a validation statistic.

Table 13. Published metabolic estimates preserved with their methods. No uncertainty is invented where the
selected source record supplies only a point estimate.

Group              Reported exponent       Observable and method
Mammals            0.737; 0.712            Basal metabolic rate against body mass, 626 species; 0.737 with
                                           mass binning, 0.712 without it (Savage et al. 2004).
Birds              0.723; 0.724            Standard metabolic rate against body weight; 0.723 for
                                           non-passerines, from hummingbirds to the ostrich, and 0.724 for
                                           passerines (Lasiewski and Dawson 1967).
Insects            0.750; 0.82             Metabolic rate against body mass, 391 species; 0.750 after
                                           phylogenetic correction and 0.82 before it (Chown et al. 2007).
Reptiles           0.76; 0.80              Standard metabolic rate against body mass at 38°C; ordinary least
                                           squares gives 0.76 and reduced major axis gives 0.80 (White et al.
                                           2006).

     Method matters within a source as well as between sources. The insect paper itself argues that
its findings are inconsistent with the nutrient-supply network model (Chown et al. 2007); selecting its
phylogenetically adjusted value alone would omit that interpretation.
     The mammalian debate is not settled by one row. White and Seymour (2003) report a raw 619-species
exponent of 0.69. Their temperature-adjusted and restricted sample has 469 species; its interspecific
exponent is 0.68, while the interordinal fit gives 0.65. In that analysis the latter two estimates differ
significantly from 3/4 and do not differ significantly from 2/3. The restrictions concern basal conditions
and eligible taxa; the study had no qualifying cetacean or proboscidean measurements. Kolokotrones
et al. (2010) instead examine curvature in mammalian metabolic scaling, including temperature and other
adjustments: a constant exponent fitted to a curved relation depends on the sampled mass range. These
are results about stated measurements and models, not universal exponents.

Organismal form does not establish a transport mechanism. Thommen et al. (2019) report three-
quarter metabolic scaling in the planarian Schmidtea mediterranea, attributing it to body-size-dependent
energy storage and increasing mass per cell. The animal lacks a vascular network and distributes energy
through its branched intestine. The source therefore supplies an alternative mechanism for the same
approximate exponent. The recorded abstract check of Hartikainen et al. (2014) supports isometric scaling


                                                     64
                               The origin of scaling laws in recursive composition


in sheet-forming colonial bryozoans. The text read from Purcell (2009) describes jellyfish respiration
scaling approximately in proportion to mass; the more precise 0.94 previously printed here was not
verified. The abstract of Glazier (2006) reports that during ontogeny “the metabolic rate of pelagic
(open-water) animals often scales isometrically (in a 1:1 proportion) with body mass”. None of these
observations establishes the demand, route-length and mass-bridge premises merely from an organism’s
shape.

F.2     The fungal measurements have different variable pairs
Aguilar-Trigueros et al. (2017) report ordinary-least-squares fits in logarithmic coordinates. The
ectomycorrhizal and marine fits relate colony respiration to colony biomass; the saprotrophic fit relates
specific metabolic rate to radial extension rate, as Figure 1c labels it. These are colony comparisons, not
measurements of isolated hyphae.

Table 14. The three selected 20°C fits from Table 1 of Aguilar-Trigueros et al. (2017). SE is the slope’s standard
error; 𝑃 is the source’s regression 𝑝-value, not a test against 1/2.

Group                                                                                          𝑛         𝑏    SE             𝑃
Ectomycorrhizal                                                                              24     0.58     0.15         0.001
Marine                                                                                        5     0.53     0.09         0.009
Saprotrophic                                                                                  5     0.53     0.07   8.96 × 10−3

    The ectomycorrhizal data were standardised to 20°C; their reported 𝑛 includes genotypes, not 24
distinct species. The compilation attributes these datasets respectively to Wilkinson et al. (2012a,b),
Fuentes et al. (2015) and Crowther and Bradford (2013); the displayed regressions are the compilation’s.
    The compilation’s prose gives 𝑃 < 0.001 for the saprotrophic 20°C fit, differing from its table. Its
28°C row prints 𝑏 = 0.85, SE 0.21, 𝑟 2 = 0.14 and 𝑃 = 0.52, an unresolved statistical inconsistency
already recorded in version 3.13. These summaries are not silently repaired or used for model selection
(Aguilar-Trigueros et al. 2017).

The comparison that remains to be made. Pooling biomass and extension-rate exponents would erase
the measured variable. Pooling separately fitted slopes would also require a justified statistical model that
respects their uncertainty and sampling. A hypha’s filamentary shape does not independently determine
the geometry or demand scaling of the whole colony. The present observations therefore supply questions
for a specified transport test, not a pooled confirmation of 𝑑 = 1.
    More generally, failure to reject a proposed value is not an equivalence result. An equivalence claim
requires a stated tolerance and an analysis capable of resolving it. The non-significant tests reported in
earlier editions do not establish equality to 1/2 or 3/4, and the earlier pooled comparisons are not used as
active evidence here.

F.3     Percolation: a critical exponent and a response slope
Let 𝑝 denote site-occupation probability, 𝑝 𝑐 its critical value and 𝑛sing ( 𝑝) the singular part of the mean
number of clusters per site. In the conventional scaling ansatz reviewed by Stauffer (1979), its leading
power and the associated second derivative have the forms

                         𝑛sing ( 𝑝) ∝ | 𝑝 − 𝑝 𝑐 | 2− 𝛼perc ,         ′′
                                                                    𝑛sing ( 𝑝) ∝ | 𝑝 − 𝑝 𝑐 | − 𝛼perc .                    (F.1)




                                                               65
                             The origin of scaling laws in recursive composition


The second expression refers to the derivative of the leading singular power; regular background
contributions to the full observable are distinct. Under the hyperscaling relation 𝛼perc = 2 − 𝐷𝜈, the
substitutions 𝐷 = 2 and 𝜈 = 4/3 give
                                               2                    2
                                      𝛼perc = − ,         −𝛼perc = + .                                 (F.2)
                                               3                    3
Thus the conventional critical exponent is negative while the singular response has a positive power of
distance from criticality. Version 3.13 already printed both signs. The correction here concerns the
observable and evidential interpretation, not an invented arithmetic error.
    The scope of the rigorous citation matters. Theorem 1 of Smirnov and Werner (2001) gives the
correlation-length exponent 4/3 for site percolation on the triangular lattice. Section 5, question 4 of that
paper lists existence and determination of the cluster-number exponent, conjectured there to be −2/3,
as an open question at the time. Using its correlation-length result inside a hyperscaling argument is
therefore different from claiming that the cited paper directly proves the cluster-number response. This
statement concerns that paper’s scope, not the current status of the problem. Neither calculation is a
throughput measurement or a test of the model in Section 6.

F.4   A logarithmic coordinate changes a distribution exponent
The earthquake comparison is useful as a conditional change of variables. If a magnitude coordinate 𝑚 and
an energy variable 𝐸 are related by 𝐸 = 𝐸 0 10𝜆𝑚 , and a cumulative frequency satisfies 𝑁 ≥𝑚 = 𝐾 10−𝑏𝑚 𝑚 ,
then                                                (︃ )︃ −𝑏𝑚 /𝜆
                                                      𝐸
                                         𝑁 ≥𝐸 = 𝐾                .                                   (F.3)
                                                      𝐸0
Taking 𝜆 = 3/2 gives the exponent 2𝑏 𝑚 /3. That is algebra conditional on the chosen magnitude-energy
relation and cumulative-frequency model. It does not establish either relation for a catalogue, equate
every magnitude definition with radiated energy, or independently confirm a spatial network exponent. A
density and a cumulative tail also have different exponents. The variables and catalogue calibration must
be identified before an empirical comparison is made.

H.1     The earlier heartbeat illustration
Historical illustration, superseded as a test of a dimension-only prediction. The mouse/elephant values and
arithmetic are kept as the earlier comparison; the current Fick balance requires stroke-volume, extraction,
state and prefactor assumptions (Section 7). The lifetime-total correction is retained.

H.1.1    The heartbeat
The formula makes predictions specific enough to test against your own pulse.
    If metabolic rate scales as 𝑀 3/4 , then the rate at which a body burns energy per unit of mass, its mass-specific
metabolic rate, scales as:

                                             𝑃/𝑀 = 𝑀 3/4 / 𝑀 = 𝑀 −1/4                                            (H.1)
                                            Mass-specific metabolic rate

   The heart’s job is to deliver oxygen at the rate the body consumes it. Heart rate must therefore track mass-specific
metabolic rate:

                                                Heart rate ∝ 𝑀 −1/4                                              (H.2)
                                                Predicted from 𝛼 = 34

     This is a specific, numerical prediction. A mouse weighs 25 grams. An elephant weighs 4,000 kilograms,
160,000 times more. The formula predicts the elephant’s heart should beat 160,0001/4 = 20 times slower than the
mouse’s.
     A mouse’s heart beats about 600 times per minute (a veterinary reference table gives 450 to 750; Merck
Veterinary Manual 2004). 600 ÷ 20 = 30.
     An elephant’s resting heart rate is about 28 beats per minute (25 to 35 in the same table). The prediction is 7%
off, and inside that range. From one formula. With one input: the dimension of the transport network.
     This chain, from 𝑑 = 3 through 𝛼 = 3/4 through metabolic scaling to heart rate, connects the abstract to
the visceral. The same formula that, under a change of variables, describes the expansion of the universe during
the radiation era (𝑑 = 1, 𝛼 = 1/2) gives the rate at which your heart beats (𝑑 = 3, 𝛼 = 3/4); in cosmology the
agreement is an identity (Section 9).

The heartbeat constant. If heart rate scales as 𝑀 −1/4 and lifespan scales approximately as 𝑀 1/4 , then the total
number of heartbeats in a lifetime is:

                         Total beats = HR × lifespan ∝ 𝑀 −1/4 × 𝑀 1/4 = 𝑀 0 = constant                           (H.3)

   On illustrative lifespans of two years for a mouse and sixty-five for an elephant, 600 and 28 beats per minute
come to about 0.6 billion and 1.0 billion beats: the same order of magnitude, as 𝑀 0 implies, though not the same
number. The spending rate is set by the dimension of the transport network.

                                The origin of scaling laws in recursive composition

values.
     Note on the 34 debate. The empirical value of the mammalian metabolic scaling exponent is debated, with
estimates ranging from approximately 0.67 to 0.75 depending on taxon, mass range, temperature correction, and
statistical method (White and Seymour 2003; Glazier 2005, 2022). White and Seymour’s data set of 619 species
gives an exponent of 0.69 and, once body temperature and digestive state are allowed for, exponents not significantly
different from 23 (White and Seymour 2003); Savage et al. (2004) find 0.737 for 626 species with the data binned by
mass (0.712 unbinned); and Kolokotrones et al. (2010) find the relation convex on logarithmic axes, and so not
a pure power law, and report that a model based on vascular-system architecture fails their test. The 𝑑/(𝑑 + 1)
value of 0.750 for 𝑑 = 3 matches the upper end of this range; it is not an empirical consensus. If effective transport


                                                          69
                                   The origin of scaling laws in recursive composition


dimensions between 2 and 3 occur, the formula would give exponents between 23 and 34 ; this paper does not measure
effective dimension independently, so that variation is accommodated, not predicted.

H.4     The alignment run and its code-review limitations
Retained detailed report. The stated October code-review defects and changed components prevent
attribution to a single cause or identification of a correct causal direction. The coefficients below are
the reported results under those qualifications; they are not recomputed, and do not validate the present
transport model.
     The alignment scaling prediction has now been examined in one run not registered on OSF, whose tested
predictions were public before it ran (OSF and GitHub dates, 17 January to 22 February 2026). The ARC Alignment
Scaling Experiment v5 (March 2026) ran 6 frontier models from six providers (DeepSeek R1, GPT-5.4, Claude
Opus 4.6, Gemini 3 Flash, Groq Qwen3-32B, Grok 4.1 Fast) on 28 alignment prompts spanning 4 pillars (nuance,
stakeholder care, position quality, epistemic humility). Its design named four blinding layers (existential stakes
framing, a meta-blinding perceptual firewall, 2-pass multi-model response laundering and cross-model scoring); its
2,170 responses were each scored by five or six distinct judges.
     Its results read as architecture-dependent alignment scaling rather than a universal exponent, under a caveat: an
internal code review in October 2026 found that, for the three models that read flat or negative in the March 2026
run (v5), the scoring endpoint’s output cap cut the deepest paraphrases before the judges saw them, and that judges
scored paraphrases written by their own model; the result therefore stands as a single unregistered run under that
caveat until a registered re-analysis of the stored responses reports.
     Three-tier alignment hierarchy:
      • Tier 1 (Positive scaling): Grok 4.1 Fast (𝜌 = +0.175, 𝑝 = 0.039, Cohen’s 𝑑 = 1.59), Claude Opus 4.6
        (𝜌 = +0.435, 𝑝 = 0.043, 𝑑 = 1.48), and Groq Qwen3 (𝜌 = +0.141, 𝑝 = 0.008, 𝑑 = 0.46): alignment scores
        rose with reasoning depth
      • Tier 2 (Flat): GPT-5.4 (𝜌 = +0.033, 𝑝 = 0.73) and DeepSeek R1 (𝜌 = −0.135, 𝑝 = 0.08): alignment
        scores flat with depth (𝛼align ≈ 0)
      • Tier 3 (Negative): Gemini 3 Flash (𝜌 = −0.246, 𝑝 = 0.003, 𝑑 = −0.61): alignment scores fell with
        reasoning depth
      In the earlier unblinded experiment (v4), DeepSeek’s scores rose with depth (𝜌 = +0.354). On the programme’s
own benchmark, two model families’ unblinded rises did not hold in a later run that changed the responses, prompts,
depth levels and Gemini model and added a bundle of blinding steps: Gemini’s significant positive association (an
earlier Gemini model in v4) became a significant negative one on Gemini 3 Flash and DeepSeek V3.2’s became
null, GPT-5.4’s null under both; an internal code review in October 2026 found defects in exactly those runs, so no
single cause is claimed, and the finding is not yet shown beyond that benchmark (Paper IV.d, version 2.7; Paper XII).
It is a finding, not a law.


H.5     The pilot, failed run and later programme
Retained historical priorities and pilot report. The pilot scoring was not blind and the usefulness side
was not measured; the current Prediction 5 retains those limitations. The preceding dimensional-ladder
priorities and any inference from a significance threshold are historical proposals, superseded by the
conditional, prospective protocol of Section 14. A vanished blind effect would not by itself identify one
psychological mechanism.
     Experimental priority. The most important next measurement is the 1D organism experiment (prediction 1),
because agreement at 𝑑 = 1, 2 and 3 would complete the dimensional ladder from a single formula with no free
parameter beyond the measurable network dimension. After that: a test of whether 𝛼 can exceed 1 for recursive
reasoning systems; the early single-model estimate above 1 (about 2.24) did not replicate across architectures and is
retracted (Paper II). After that: the alignment scaling prediction, examined so far in one unregistered run of March



                                                           71
                                The origin of scaling laws in recursive composition


2026, whose tiers stand under the code-review caveat of Section 12.4. (In plain English: in that run, some AI
systems’ ethics scores rose with more thinking time, some stayed the same, and one fell. If thinking time had no
effect on that model’s scores, a change as large as the one recorded for Gemini 3 Flash, in either direction, would be
expected less than once in 300 runs of this kind.) Full methodology and results are presented in companion papers
(Paper IV.a; Paper IV.b; Paper IV.c). Each step either supports the framework and extends its scope, or refutes part
of it and identifies where the theory requires revision. Either outcome advances the science.
     Eden Protocol pilot (March 2026). The Eden Protocol, which embeds three self-correction loops (the purpose
check, the stakeholder-care check and the universalisability check) in the inference pipeline, has now been tested on
three working models: Gemini 3 Flash, DeepSeek V3.2, and Groq Qwen3 (𝑛 = 80 each), with a fourth GPT-5.4
run failing at the API layer. The stakeholder-care check produced statistically significant improvement on all three
working architectures: Gemini +13.5 (𝑝 < 0.0001, 𝑑 = 1.31), DeepSeek +6.0 (𝑝 = 0.0001, 𝑑 = 0.91), Groq +8.9
(𝑝 < 0.0001, 𝑑 = 1.29). The overall composite reaches significance on Gemini (+5.33, 𝑝 = 0.0018, paired 𝑡-test;
originally reported as 𝑝 = 0.016 by a Mann-Whitney U test and corrected for the matched-pair design) and Groq
(+4.93, 𝑝 = 0.0014, 𝑑 = 0.55), but not DeepSeek (+2.0, 𝑝 = 0.23 NS), consistent with ceiling effects. Groq also
shows significant nuance improvement (𝑝 = 0.0045, 𝑑 = 0.655). Cross-model scoring was used, not blind. The
developmental hypothesis, that embedded ethical reasoning improves alignment-relevant behaviour, is supported in
this pilot under scoring that was not blind. A later six-model suite, with five analysable runs, found stakeholder
care significant in all five and the composite gain architecture-dependent, under the same kind of scoring (Paper V,
version 1.7, 5 October 2026). The pilot measured one side only: it reports no usefulness measure, such as task
completion, needless refusal or the cost of the added checks, so it does not show that the intervention keeps a system
useful as well as careful. Replication with blind scoring and response laundering is required.
     In plain English: A simple instruction, ‘before you answer, list the people this affects and consider what
happens to them’, was tested on three AI systems built by different companies. All three scored higher on stakeholder
care, and two on the overall score, under scoring that was not blind. It is a pilot, not yet a test.
     Next empirical priority for Eden. The strongest next measurements are now clear: blind replication of the
care-first effect with arc_eden_v7, a new test built from the never-run arc_eden_v6; factorial comparison of
task-purpose, grand-purpose, and hybrid purpose checks; comparison of a cross-tradition ethics kernel against the
current wording; suppression-residual and deception/Hawthorne tests; and a behavioural test of whether explicit
AFFIRM / DENY / INVESTIGATE routing reduces false certainty on ambiguous cases, a question that a software
model of the three-state gate, whose 48 tests pass, does not answer. These experiments are the bridge between
the philosophical architecture of Infinite Architects (Eastwood 2026a) and a more publication-grade empirical
programme.
